[03:34:54] When most people hear the word
[03:34:56] valuation, they think about benchmarks.
[03:34:58] A model scores 90% on a benchmark. A new
[03:35:01] version scores 92%. The team celebrates.
[03:35:04] But agent systems have fundamentally
[03:35:06] changed what the evaluation means. Today
[03:35:09] the systems don't simply generate
[03:35:10] answers. They plan, they call tools,
[03:35:13] they retrieve information. They execute
[03:35:14] workflows. They interact with the
[03:35:16] production infrastructure. The question
[03:35:18] is no longer did the model generate the
[03:35:20] right answer. The question is did the
[03:35:22] system behave correctly. Today I would
[03:35:25] like to discuss how evaluation is
[03:35:27] evolving from model benchmarking into
[03:35:29] production infrastructure.
[03:35:35] This is the problem almost every AI
[03:35:37] organization is encountering today.
[03:35:39] Offline benchmarks continue improving.
[03:35:41] Yet production reliability often remains
[03:35:43] unpredictable. Why is that? Because
[03:35:46] benchmarks measure model capability.
[03:35:48] Production measures system behavior. A
[03:35:50] benchmark doesn't capture tool failure,
[03:35:52] API outage, context changes, user
[03:35:55] variability, longunning workflows. And
[03:35:58] as systems become more autonomous, the
[03:36:00] gap between the benchmark performance
[03:36:01] and production performance grows. The
[03:36:03] result is what many teams experience
[03:36:05] today. High benchmark scores as you can
[03:36:08] see, but unreliable production behavior.
[03:36:14] Traditional evaluation focus on outputs.
[03:36:17] But we should ask the question, did the
[03:36:19] model produce a correct answer? Agentic
[03:36:21] systems force us to ask a different
[03:36:23] question. Did the system behave
[03:36:24] correctly? Behavior includes planning
[03:36:27] quality, tool usage, execution, workflow
[03:36:30] execution, recovery from failures,
[03:36:32] decision making. In other words, we are
[03:36:35] moving from evaluating answers to
[03:36:37] evaluating workflows. And that requires
[03:36:39] fundamentally different evaluation
[03:36:41] architectures.
[03:36:43] Many teams still think hallucinations
[03:36:45] are the primary AI failure modes. In
[03:36:47] production, they are often just one
[03:36:49] category. Agentic systems introduce an
[03:36:52] entire hierarchy of failure modes. At
[03:36:54] the very foundation the memory failures,
[03:36:57] retrieable failures, safety failures. As
[03:36:59] you go up, you have to think about
[03:37:01] reasoning mistakes, poor planning,
[03:37:03] incorrect tool execution. At the highest
[03:37:05] layer, you have to think about multi-
[03:37:06] aent coordination failures. And this is
[03:37:08] why evaluating only model output misses
[03:37:12] the most production risks we observe.
[03:37:15] One of the most useful mindset shifts is
[03:37:17] to stop thinking like researchers and
[03:37:20] start thinking like a SR or a production
[03:37:22] engineer. S SR don't measure success
[03:37:25] using accuracy. They measure
[03:37:26] reliability, availability, latency, cost
[03:37:28] recovery and agentic systems require the
[03:37:31] same approach. The goal is not
[03:37:33] maximizing the benchmark scores. The
[03:37:35] goal is to maximize dependable outcomes.
[03:37:37] Reliability becomes the northstar
[03:37:39] metric. Accuracy becomes the only input.
[03:37:47] In this pyramid is how I think
[03:37:49] personally think about modern AI
[03:37:50] evaluation systems. At the bottom you
[03:37:52] can see there are benchmarks. They're
[03:37:54] useful. They're scalable. They're
[03:37:55] reputable. But the operational value is
[03:37:57] limited. In the middle there scenario
[03:37:59] based valuations. These simulate
[03:38:01] realistic workflows. And at the very top
[03:38:04] you see production telemetry. This is
[03:38:06] where the highest value valuation

[03:38:23] evaluating prompts we evaluate
[03:38:25] scenarios. For example, a customer

[03:38:28] workflow, a research workflow. The agent

[03:39:12] Many organizations view humans as

[03:39:29] blind spots in automated metrics. The
[03:39:32] most successful systems combine
[03:39:33] automated valuation with targeted human

[03:39:38] Now, agent systems drift constantly.
